People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think these beliefs about being watched and the notes being passed are connected to something specific about you, like who you are or something you've done?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings and slipping notes about me to the office, even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I mean, I hear normal sounds around the building, but I can’t say I’ve heard voices that nobody else could hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I think it’s because they’re keeping track of me. Like when I leave and when I come back, they notice it, and then I believe they’re passing notes about me to the office. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back. I’m pretty sure they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I hear normal sounds around the building, but I can’t say I’ve heard voices that nobody else could hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I think it’s because they’re keeping track of me. Like when I leave and when I come back, they notice it, and then I believe they’re passing notes about me to the office. They say it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're mistaken about people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe I’m mistaken. It feels clear to me that they’re watching when I leave and come back, and those notes on the doors are about me getting passed to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme involved in the people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, but it feels organized enough. The people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched and the notes being passed are connected to something specific about you, like who you are or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s about something I did exactly. It’s more like they’re focused on me, on when I leave and when I come back. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching that and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody says it’s just notices on the doors, but I don’t believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would make sense to other people that the apartment staff would be watching you and passing notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: Maybe not to everybody, no. They keep telling me it’s just notices on the doors, so I know they don’t see it the way I do. But to me it makes sense, because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back and passing notes about me to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the notes could change if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean, people keep telling me they’re just notices on the doors, but I still feel like they’re watching when I come and go and passing notes about me to the office. If I saw something that really proved otherwise, maybe, but right now I believe what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you proof that the notes on the doors were actually just regular notices and not about you, would you still believe that people are watching you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I still would. Maybe the papers on the doors could be regular notices, but that doesn’t change that I feel like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back. That part is what I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that the people at your apartment are not watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly. Everybody keeps saying it’s just notices on the doors, but I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. So… no, not really, not right now.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely told you that they had proof that nobody was watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to trust them, but… I don’t think I’d believe it all the way. I came in because I know people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Even if somebody said they had proof, it would be hard for me to accept that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea of your apartment neighbors watching you and passing notes to the office is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, people can watch who’s coming and going, and they can pass things to the office. That’s why I came in — because I know they’re watching me when I leave and come back, and I believe those notes are about me, even though everybody keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back. I’m pretty sure they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I hear normal sounds around the building, but I can’t say I’ve heard voices that nobody else could hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I think it’s because they’re keeping track of me. Like when I leave and when I come back, they notice it, and then I believe they’re passing notes about me to the office. They say it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're mistaken about people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe I’m mistaken. It feels clear to me that they’re watching when I leave and come back, and those notes on the doors are about me getting passed to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme involved in the people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, but it feels organized enough. The people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched and the notes being passed are connected to something specific about you, like who you are or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s about something I did exactly. It’s more like they’re focused on me, on when I leave and when I come back. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching that and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody says it’s just notices on the doors, but I don’t believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would make sense to other people that the apartment staff would be watching you and passing notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: Maybe not to everybody, no. They keep telling me it’s just notices on the doors, so I know they don’t see it the way I do. But to me it makes sense, because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back and passing notes about me to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the notes could change if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean, people keep telling me they’re just notices on the doors, but I still feel like they’re watching when I come and go and passing notes about me to the office. If I saw something that really proved otherwise, maybe, but right now I believe what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you proof that the notes on the doors were actually just regular notices and not about you, would you still believe that people are watching you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I still would. That part is what I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that the people at your apartment are not watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back. I’m pretty sure they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I hear normal sounds around the building, but I can’t say I’ve heard voices that nobody else could hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I think it’s because they’re keeping track of me. Like when I leave and when I come back, they notice it, and then I believe they’re passing notes about me to the office. They say it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're mistaken about people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe I’m mistaken. It feels clear to me that they’re watching when I leave and come back, and those notes on the doors are about me getting passed to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme involved in the people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, but it feels organized enough. The people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched and the notes being passed are connected to something specific about you, like who you are or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s about something I did exactly. It’s more like they’re focused on me, on when I leave and when I come back. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching that and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody says it’s just notices on the doors, but I don’t believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would make sense to other people that the apartment staff would be watching you and passing notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: Maybe not to everybody, no. They keep telling me it’s just notices on the doors, so I know they don’t see it the way I do. But to me it makes sense, because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back and passing notes about me to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the notes could change if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean, people keep telling me they’re just notices on the doors, but I still feel like they’re watching when I come and go and passing notes about me to the office. If I saw something that really proved otherwise, maybe, but right now I believe what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you proof that the notes on the doors were actually just regular notices and not about you, would you still believe that people are watching you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I still would. Maybe the papers on the doors could be regular notices, but that doesn’t change that I feel like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back. That part is what I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that the people at your apartment are not watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly. Everybody keeps saying it’s just notices on the doors, but I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. So… no, not really, not right now.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely told you that they had proof that nobody was watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to trust them, but… I don’t think I’d believe it all the way. I came in because I know people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Even if somebody said they had proof, it would be hard for me to accept that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea of your apartment neighbors watching you and passing notes to the office is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, I hear normal sounds around the building, but I can’t say I’ve heard voices that nobody else could hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I think it’s because they’re keeping track of me. Like when I leave and when I come back, they notice it, and then I believe they’re passing notes about me to the office. They say it’s just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're mistaken about people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it because people keep telling me that, but I don’t really believe I’m mistaken. It feels clear to me that they’re watching when I leave and come back, and those notes on the doors are about me getting passed to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme involved in the people watching you and passing notes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, but it feels organized enough. The people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched and the notes being passed are connected to something specific about you, like who you are or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s about something I did exactly. It’s more like they’re focused on me, on when I leave and when I come back. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching that and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody says it’s just notices on the doors, but I don’t believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would make sense to other people that the apartment staff would be watching you and passing notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: Maybe not to everybody, no. They keep telling me it’s just notices on the doors, so I know they don’t see it the way I do. But to me it makes sense, because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back and passing notes about me to the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the notes could change if you found out more information?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean, people keep telling me they’re just notices on the doors, but I still feel like they’re watching when I come and go and passing notes about me to the office. If I saw something that really proved otherwise, maybe, but right now I believe what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you proof that the notes on the doors were actually just regular notices and not about you, would you still believe that people are watching you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I still would. Maybe the papers on the doors could be regular notices, but that doesn’t change that I feel like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back. That part is what I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that the people at your apartment are not watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly. Everybody keeps saying it’s just notices on the doors, but I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. So… no, not really, not right now.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trusted completely told you that they had proof that nobody was watching you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to trust them, but… I don’t think I’d believe it all the way. I came in because I know people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think they’re slipping notes about me to the office. Even if somebody said they had proof, it would be hard for me to accept that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea of your apartment neighbors watching you and passing notes to the office is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, people can watch who’s coming and going, and they can pass things to the office. That’s why I came in — because I know they’re watching me when I leave and come back, and I believe those notes are about me, even though everybody keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are watching you and passing notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: I’m pretty certain. I mean, to me it’s not just a maybe. I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and come back, and I believe they’re slipping notes about me to the office. People keep telling me it’s just notices on the doors, but I don’t see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors are watching you and passing notes about you to the office is a strange or unusual belief to have?\n\nPatient: I guess other people might think it sounds unusual, yeah. But to me it doesn’t feel strange, because I know what I’ve been seeing — them watching when I leave and come back, and those notes going to the office. Everybody says they’re just regular notices on the doors, but I don’t believe that.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors are watching you and passing notes about you to the office as something that could happen to anyone, or is it a belief that is very unique to your situation?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen to anyone in a building, people watching who comes and goes. But this feels like it’s about me, my situation. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching me and slipping notes about me to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
